Output

<path>/source/head/.cursor/rules/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

<path>/source/head/.cursor/rules/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

Also check how `EndpoointsWithParked` is used, and whether iol's `ensureNumSlotsEnv`/`GenInterfaceConfig` correctly handle parked endpoints (parked endpoints have iface names? Let me check clablinks.ParkingNode).

Key potential issue: `EndpointsWithParked` calls `d.parkingNode.GetEndpoints()` without a lock while `d.Endpoints` is normally accessed... probably fine.

Another potential issue: in `GenInterfaceConfig` in iol.go, the parked endpoints may have interface names like "parked" or something — need to check what ParkingNode endpoints' GetIfaceName returns. If parked endpoints have no linux iface name (they're parked in a netns), including them in GenInterfaceConfig could miscompute slot/port or generate invalid config. Similarly `ensureNumSlotsEnv` counting parked endpoints might inflate needed slots — but that could be intentional: parked interfaces still occupy IOL slot mapping? Actually the point of the PR seems: CLAB_INTFS must count parked endpoints so vrnetlab waits for all interfaces; and IOL interface config should include parked endpoints so their mapping is preserved across park/restore cycles.

Wait — but for GenInterfaceConfig, the function iterates endpoints and generates iouyap netmap config from `intf.GetIfaceName()`. If parked endpoints' iface names differ (e.g. contain "parked" prefix?), the regexp `\d+` might misparse. Need to read clablinks parking node code.

Also the docs change: telnet console port 5000 on management address. Need to verify implementation matches? The PR doesn't change iol code to serve console — the doc says images built after November 2026 serve console. Hmm, doc-only claim about vrnetlab images. Check schema for something? Not relevant.

Also check the boot_config.txt change: previously always recreated (truncating); now only created if not exists. Why? Possibly because boot_config.txt holds IOS config and being recreated wiped it. Behavior change: on redeploy, boot_config.txt previously reset; now persists. Is that intentional? Could be intentional fix. But maybe there's a subtlety: `CreateFile` with empty content when the file exists with content — old behavior truncated boot_config.txt each deploy; new behavior preserves. Probably intentional (IOL saves startup config to boot_config.txt?). Actually boot_config.txt in IOL is the startup-config file. Truncating it on every deploy would wipe saved config on redeploy/restart — that's the bug being fixed. Good.

Now let's read iol.go head fully around relevant sections, and the ParkingNode in clablinks. Also check linux.go and vr_node? Let me find clablinks ParkingNode.

168: 	trackedEndpoints := src.GetEndpoints()
169: 	tracked := make(map[string]Endpoint, len(trackedEndpoints))
170: 	for _, ep := range trackedEndpoints {
171: 		tracked[ep.GetIfaceName()] = ep
172: 	}
173: 	presentIfaceNames, err := listOwnedInterfaceNames(ctx, src, trackedIfaceNames(trackedEndpoints))
174: 	if err != nil {
175: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
176: 			"failed to discover runtime interfaces for node %q: %w",
177: 			src.GetShortName(),
178: 			err,
179: 		)
180: 	}
181: 
182: 	endpoints := make([]Endpoint, 0, len(presentIfaceNames))
183: 	for _, ifaceName := range presentIfaceNames {
184: 		ep, ok := tracked[ifaceName]
185: 		if !ok {
186: 			ep = NewRuntimeEndpoint(src, ifaceName)
187: 			if err := src.AdoptEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
188: 				return nil, err
189: 			}
190: 		}
191: 
192: 		endpoints = append(endpoints, ep)
193: 	}
194: 
195: 	return endpoints, nil
196: }
197: 
198: func trackedIfaceNames(endpointSets ...[]Endpoint) map[string]struct{} {
199: 	ifaceNames := map[string]struct{}{}
200: 
201: 	for _, endpoints := range endpointSets {
202: 		for _, ep := range endpoints {
203: 			ifaceNames[ep.GetIfaceName()] = struct{}{}
204: 		}
205: 	}
206: 
207: 	return ifaceNames
208: }
209: 
210: func listOwnedInterfaceNames(
211: 	ctx context.Context,
212: 	node Node,
213: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},
214: ) ([]string, error) {
215: 	var ifaces []string
216: 
217: 	err := node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
218: 		links, err := netlink.LinkList()
219: 		if err != nil {
220: 			return err
221: 		}
222: 
223: 		for _, link := range links {
224: 			if !isOwnedInterface(link, knownIfaceNames) {
225: 				continue
226: 			}
227: 
228: 			ifaces = append(ifaces, link.Attrs().Name)
229: 		}
230: 
231: 		return nil
232: 	})
233: 	if err != nil {
234: 		return nil, err
235: 	}
236: 
237: 	slices.Sort(ifaces)
238: 
239: 	return ifaces, nil
240: }
241: 
242: func isOwnedInterface(link netlink.Link, knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{}) bool {
243: 	name := link.Attrs().Name
244: 	switch name {
245: 	case "lo", "eth0":
246: 		return false
247: 	}
248: 
249: 	if _, known := knownIfaceNames[name]; !known && !hasOwnershipAltName(link) {
250: 		return false
251: 	}
252: 
253: 	return true
254: }
255: 
256: func (*ParkingNode) GetLinkEndpointType() LinkEndpointType {
257: 	return LinkEndpointTypeVeth
258: }

117: func (n *iol) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
118: 	// Init DefaultNode
119: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)

125: 	}

131: 		pid += *off
132: 	}

135: 
136: 	env := map[string]string{
137: 		"IOL_PID": n.Pid,
138: 	}
139: 
140: 	n.Cfg.Env = clabutils.MergeStringMaps(env, n.Cfg.Env)

142: 	// check if user submitted node type is valid
143: 	switch nodeType {
144: 	case "":
145: 		n.isL2Node = strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(path.Base(n.Cfg.Image)), "l2")
146: 	case typeIOL:
147: 		n.isL2Node = false
148: 	case typeL2:
149: 		n.isL2Node = true
150: 	default:
151: 		return fmt.Errorf("invalid node type '%s'. Valid types are: %s",
152: 			n.Cfg.NodeType, strings.Join(validTypes, ", "))
153: 	}
154: 
155: 	if err := n.parseMgmtIntf(); err != nil {
156: 		return err
157: 	}
158: 	n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_MGMT_INTF"] = n.mgmtIntf
159: 
160: 	n.nvramFile = fmt.Sprint("nvram_", fmt.Sprintf("%05s", n.Pid))
161:

163: 		// mount nvram so that config persists
164: 		fmt.Sprint(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile), ":", path.Join(iol_workdir, n.nvramFile)),

177: // parseMgmtIntf parses the mgmt-intf kind-specific config key and normalizes it to
178: // Ethernet<slot>/<port>;
179: // empty means the default Ethernet0/0.
180: func (n *iol) parseMgmtIntf() error {
181: 	if v := n.kindSpecificCfg().MgmtIntf; v != "" {
182: 		captureGroups, err := clabutils.GetRegexpCaptureGroups(CapturingIntfRegexp, v)
183: 		if err != nil {
184: 			return fmt.Errorf("invalid mgmt-intf %q: %w\n%s", v, err, IntfHelpMsg)
185: 		}
186: 
187: 		n.mgmtSlot, _ = strconv.Atoi(captureGroups["slot"])
188: 		n.mgmtPort, _ = strconv.Atoi(captureGroups["port"])
189: 
190: 		if n.mgmtSlot > 9 || n.mgmtPort > 3 {
191: 			return fmt.Errorf(
192: 				"invalid mgmt-intf %q: slot must be 0-9 and port must be 0-3", v)
193: 		}
194: 	}
195: 
196: 	n.mgmtIntf = fmt.Sprintf("Ethernet%d/%d", n.mgmtSlot, n.mgmtPort)
197: 	n.mgmtLinuxIdx = n.mgmtSlot*4 + n.mgmtPort
198: 
199: 	return nil
200: }
201: 
202: // swapMgmtIdx swaps linux interface index 0 with the management interface's index,
203: // so Ethernet0/0 takes the index a relocated management interface vacated.
204: func (n *iol) swapMgmtIdx(idx int) int {
205: 	switch idx {
206: 	case 0:
207: 		return n.mgmtLinuxIdx
208: 	case n.mgmtLinuxIdx:
209: 		return 0
210: 	}
211: 
212: 	return idx
213: }
214: 
215: // iolPortForLinuxIdx returns the IOL slot/port backed by linux interface index idx.
216: func (n *iol) iolPortForLinuxIdx(idx int) (slot, port int) {
217: 	mapped := n.swapMgmtIdx(idx)
218: 	return mapped / 4, mapped % 4
219: }

221: // ensureNumSlotsEnv exports CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS when the management interface is
222: // relocated, so that its slot exists even when no link lands in it.
223: func (n *iol) ensureNumSlotsEnv() {
224: 	if n.mgmtLinuxIdx == 0 || n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] != "" {
225: 		return
226: 	}
227: 
228: 	intfIdxRegexp := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
229: 	needed := n.mgmtSlot + 1
230: 
231: 	for _, ep := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {
232: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(intfIdxRegexp.FindString(ep.GetIfaceName()))
233: 		if slot, _ := n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x); slot+1 > needed {
234: 			needed = slot + 1
235: 		}
236: 	}
237: 
238: 	n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] = strconv.Itoa(needed)
239: }

241: func (n *iol) PreDeploy(ctx context.Context, params *clabnodes.PreDeployParams) error {
242: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(n.Cfg.LabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)
243: 
244: 	if v := n.kindSpecificCfg().BootstrapConfig; v != "" {
245: 		switch p := clabutils.ResolvePath(v, params.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir()); {
246: 		case strings.EqualFold(v, "none"):
247: 			n.bootstrapNone = true
248: 		case clabutils.FileExists(p):
249: 			n.bootstrapCfgFile = p
250: 		default:
251: 			return fmt.Errorf("bootstrap-config file %q does not exist", p)
252: 		}
253: 	}
254: 
255: 	_, err := n.LoadOrGenerateCertificate(params.Cert, params.TopologyName)
256: 	if err != nil {
257: 		return err
258: 	}
259: 
260: 	n.ensureNumSlotsEnv()
261: 
262: 	return n.CreateIOLFiles(ctx)
263: }

265: func (n *iol) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
266: 	log.Infof("Running postdeploy actions for Cisco IOL '%s' node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
267: 
268: 	n.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)
269: 
270: 	if err := n.GenBootConfig(ctx); err != nil {
271: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to generate boot config: %w", err)
272: 	}
273: 
274: 	// Must update mgmt IP if not first boot, unless the node boots without a baseline config
275: 	if !n.firstBoot && !n.bootstrapNone {
276: 		return n.UpdateMgmtIntf(ctx)
277: 	}
278: 
279: 	return nil
280: }
281: 
282: // Start re-applies the mgmt veth TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime
283: // recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
284: // See disableMgmtTxOffload for why IOL cannot function with it enabled.
285: func (n *iol) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
286: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
287: 		return err
288: 	}
289: 
290: 	// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix
291: 	// for them as well, as there is no container mgmt veth to adjust.
292: 	if n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
293: 		return nil
294: 	}
295: 
296: 	n.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)
297: 
298: 	return nil
299: }
300: 
301: // disableMgmtTxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the host NS veth for the mgmt interface.
302: // iouyap bridges IOL interfaces over packet sockets; frames carrying an offloaded
303: // (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum reach the IOL process with an incomplete checksum, which its
304: // TCP/IP stack drops, breaking TCP/UDP connectivity.
305: //
306: // Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,
307: // and there is no clab-created mgmt veth to adjust.
308: func (n *iol) disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
309: 	if n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "host" {
310: 		return
311: 	}
312: 
313: 	if n.Runtime.Mgmt().Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {
314: 		return
315: 	}
316: 
317: 	var peerIfIndex int
318: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.VethPeerIndex("eth0", &peerIfIndex))
319: 	if err != nil {
320: 		log.Warn("Failed to get veth peer index for IOL mgmt interface",
321: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
322: 			"error", err)
323: 		return
324: 	}
325: 
326: 	if err := clabutils.DisableTxOffloadByIndex(peerIfIndex); err != nil {
327: 		log.Warn("Failed to disable TX checksum offload on IOL mgmt host veth",
328: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
329: 			"error", err)
330: 	}
331: }
332: 
333: func (n *iol) CreateIOLFiles(ctx context.Context) error {
334: 	// If NVRAM already exists, don't need to create
335: 	// otherwise saved configs in NVRAM are overwritten.
336: 	if !clabutils.FileExists(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile)) {
337: 		// create nvram file
338: 		clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile), "")
339: 		n.firstBoot = true
340: 	}
341: 	// create these files so the bind monut doesn't automatically
342: 	// make folders.
343: 	if bootCfg := path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "boot_config.txt"); !clabutils.FileExists(bootCfg) {
344: 		clabutils.CreateFile(bootCfg, "")
345: 	}
346: 
347: 	return n.GenInterfaceConfig(ctx)
348: }

350: // Generate interfaces configuration for IOL (and iouyap/netmap).
351: func (n *iol) GenInterfaceConfig(_ context.Context) error {
352: 	// add 'boilerplate' to NETMAP and iouyap.ini for the management port
353: 	iouyapData := fmt.Sprintf(
354: 		"[default]\nbase_port = 49000\nnetmap = /iol/NETMAP\n[513:%d/%d]\neth_dev = eth0\n",
355: 		n.mgmtSlot,
356: 		n.mgmtPort,
357: 	)
358: 	netmapdata := fmt.Sprintf(
359: 		"%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\n",
360: 		n.Pid,
361: 		n.mgmtSlot,
362: 		n.mgmtPort,
363: 		n.mgmtSlot,
364: 		n.mgmtPort,
365: 	)
366: 
367: 	slot, port := 0, 0
368: 
369: 	// Regexp to pull number out of linux'ethX' interface naming
370: 	IntfRegExpr := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
371: 
372: 	for _, intf := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {
373: 		// get numeric interface number and cast to int
374: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(IntfRegExpr.FindString(intf.GetIfaceName()))
375: 
376: 		// Interface naming is Ethernet{slot}/{port}. Each slot contains max 4 ports
377: 		slot, port = n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x)
378: 
379: 		// append data to write to NETMAP and IOUYAP files
380: 		iouyapData += fmt.Sprintf("[513:%d/%d]\neth_dev = %s\n", slot, port, intf.GetIfaceName())
381: 		netmapdata += fmt.Sprintf("%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\n", n.Pid, slot, port, slot, port)
382: 
383: 		// populate template array for config
384: 		ipv4Addr := ""
385: 		ipv4Mask := ""
386: 		v4Prefix := intf.GetIPv4Addr()
387: 		ipv6Addr := ""
388: 
389: 		if v4Prefix.IsValid() {
390: 			ipv4Addr = v4Prefix.Addr().String()
391: 			ipv4Mask = clabutils.CIDRToDDN(v4Prefix.Bits())
392: 		}
393: 
394: 		if a := intf.GetIPv6Addr(); a.IsValid() {
395: 			ipv6Addr = a.String()
396: 		}
397: 
398: 		n.interfaces = append(n.interfaces,
399: 			IOLInterface{
400: 				IfaceName: intf.GetIfaceName(),
401: 				IfaceIdx:  x,
402: 				Slot:      slot,
403: 				Port:      port,
404: 				IPv4Addr:  ipv4Addr,
405: 				IPv4Mask:  ipv4Mask,
406: 				IPv6Addr:  ipv6Addr,
407: 			},
408: 		)
409: 	}
410: 
411: 	// create IOUYAP and NETMAP file for interface mappings
412: 	err := clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "iouyap.ini"), iouyapData)
413: 	if err != nil {
414: 		return err
415: 	}
416: 	err = clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "NETMAP"), netmapdata)
417: 
418: 	return err
419: }

421: func (n *iol) GenBootConfig(_ context.Context) error {
422: 	n.bootCfg = cfgTemplate
423: 
424: 	switch {
425: 	case n.bootstrapNone:
426: 		n.bootCfg = "{{ .PartialCfg }}"
427: 	case n.bootstrapCfgFile != "":
428: 		cfg, err := os.ReadFile(n.bootstrapCfgFile)
429: 		if err != nil {
430: 			return err
431: 		}
432: 
433: 		n.bootCfg = string(cfg)
434: 	}
435: 
436: 	if n.Cfg.StartupConfig != "" {
437: 		cfg, err := os.ReadFile(n.Cfg.StartupConfig)
438: 		if err != nil {
439: 			return err
440: 		}
441: 
442: 		if clabutils.IsPartialConfigFile(n.Cfg.StartupConfig) {
443: 			n.partialStartupCfg = string(cfg)
444: 		} else {
445: 			n.bootCfg = string(cfg)
446: 		}
447: 	}
448: 
449: 	// create startup config template
450: 	tpl := IOLTemplateData{
451: 		Hostname:           n.Cfg.ShortName,
452: 		IsL2Node:           n.isL2Node,
453: 		MgmtIntf:           n.mgmtIntf,
454: 		MgmtIPv4Addr:       n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Address,
455: 		MgmtIPv4SubnetMask: clabutils.CIDRToDDN(n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4PrefixLength),
456: 		MgmtIPv4GW:         n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Gateway,
457: 		MgmtIPv6Addr:       n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Address,
458: 		MgmtIPv6PrefixLen:  n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6PrefixLength,
459: 		MgmtIPv6GW:         n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Gateway,
460: 		DataIFaces:         n.interfaces,
461: 	}
462: 
463: 	// render the partial startup config so template variables work in partials too
464: 	if n.partialStartupCfg != "" {
465: 		partialTpl, err := template.New("clab-iol-partial-config").Funcs(
466: 			clabutils.CreateFuncs()).Parse(n.partialStartupCfg)
467: 		if err != nil {
468: 			return err
469: 		}
470: 
471: 		buf := new(bytes.Buffer)
472: 		if err := partialTpl.Execute(buf, tpl); err != nil {
473: 			return err
474: 		}
475: 
476: 		tpl.PartialCfg = buf.String()
477: 	}
478: 
479: 	IOLCfgTpl, err := template.New("clab-iol-default-config").Funcs(
480: 		clabutils.CreateFuncs()).Parse(n.bootCfg)
481: 	if err != nil {
482: 		return err
483: 	}
484: 
485: 	// generate the config
486: 	buf := new(bytes.Buffer)
487: 	err = IOLCfgTpl.Execute(buf, tpl)
488: 	if err != nil {
489: 		return err
490: 	}
491: 
492: 	return clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "boot_config.txt"), buf.String())
493: }

495: type IOLTemplateData struct {
496: 	Hostname           string
497: 	IsL2Node           bool
498: 	MgmtIntf           string
499: 	MgmtIPv4Addr       string
500: 	MgmtIPv4SubnetMask string
501: 	MgmtIPv4GW         string
502: 	MgmtIPv6Addr       string
503: 	MgmtIPv6PrefixLen  int
504: 	MgmtIPv6GW         string
505: 	DataIFaces         []IOLInterface
506: 	PartialCfg         string
507: }
508: 
509: // IOLInterface struct stores mapping info between
510: // IOL interface name and linux container interface.
511: type IOLInterface struct {
512: 	IfaceName string
513: 	IfaceIdx  int
514: 	Slot      int
515: 	Port      int
516: 	IPv4Addr  string
517: 	IPv4Mask  string
518: 	IPv6Addr  string
519: }
520: 
521: func (n *iol) GetMappedInterfaceName(ifName string) (string, error) {
522: 	captureGroups, err := clabutils.GetRegexpCaptureGroups(CapturingIntfRegexp, ifName)
523: 	if err != nil {
524: 		return "", err
525: 	}
526: 
527: 	indexGroups := []string{"slot", "port"}
528: 	parsedIndices := make(map[string]int)
529: 	foundIndices := make(map[string]bool)
530: 
531: 	for _, indexKey := range indexGroups {
532: 		if index, found := captureGroups[indexKey]; found && index != "" {
533: 			foundIndices[indexKey] = true
534: 			parsedIndices[indexKey], err = strconv.Atoi(index)
535: 			if err != nil {
536: 				return "", fmt.Errorf(
537: 					"%q parsed %s index %q could not be cast to an integer",
538: 					ifName,
539: 					indexKey,
540: 					index,
541: 				)
542: 			}
543: 			if parsedIndices[indexKey] < 0 {
544: 				return "", fmt.Errorf(
545: 					"%q parsed %q index %q does not match requirement >= 0",
546: 					ifName,
547: 					indexKey,
548: 					index,
549: 				)
550: 			}
551: 		} else {
552: 			foundIndices[indexKey] = false
553: 		}
554: 	}
555: 
556: 	// return an ethX interface name. Slots are in 'groups' of 4 interfaces each
557: 	if foundIndices["slot"] && foundIndices["port"] {
558: 		idx := n.swapMgmtIdx((parsedIndices["slot"] * 4) + parsedIndices["port"])
559: 		return fmt.Sprintf("eth%d", idx), nil
560: 	} else {
561: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("%q missing slot or port index", ifName)
562: 	}
563: }
564: 
565: // AddEndpoint override maps the endpoint name to an ethX-based naming where necessary, before
566: // adding it to the node endpoints. Returns an error if the mapping goes wrong or if the
567: // interface name is NOT allowed.
568: func (n *iol) AddEndpoint(e clablinks.Endpoint) error {
569: 	endpointName := e.GetIfaceName()
570: 	var IFaceName, IFaceAlias string
571: 
572: 	IFaceName = endpointName
573: 
574: 	if !(DefaultIntfRegexp.MatchString(endpointName)) &&
575: 		AllowedIntfRegexp.MatchString(endpointName) {
576: 		log.Debugf("%s: %s needs mapping", n.Cfg.ShortName, endpointName)
577: 		mappedName, err := n.GetMappedInterfaceName(endpointName)
578: 		if err != nil {
579: 			return fmt.Errorf(
580: 				"%q interface name %q could not be mapped to an ethX-based interface name: %w\n%s",
581: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
582: 				e.GetIfaceName(),
583: 				err,
584: 				IntfHelpMsg,
585: 			)
586: 		}
587: 		log.Debugf(
588: 			"Interface Mapping: Mapping interface %q (ifAlias) to %q (ifName)",
589: 			endpointName,
590: 			mappedName,
591: 		)
592: 		IFaceName = mappedName
593: 		IFaceAlias = endpointName
594: 	}
595: 
596: 	e.SetIfaceName(IFaceName)
597: 	// should be nil if ethX naming is used.
598: 	e.SetIfaceAlias(IFaceAlias)
599: 	n.Endpoints = append(n.Endpoints, e)
600: 
601: 	return nil
602: }
603: 
604: func (n *iol) CheckInterfaceName() error {
605: 	err := n.CheckInterfaceOverlap()
606: 	if err != nil {
607: 		return err
608: 	}
609: 
610: 	for _, e := range n.Endpoints {
611: 		IFaceName := e.GetIfaceName()
612: 		// eth0 always backs the management interface
613: 		if IFaceName == "eth0" {
614: 			return fmt.Errorf(
615: 				"IOL Node: %q. Management interface %s (eth0) is not allowed",
616: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
617: 				n.mgmtIntf,
618: 			)
619: 		}
620: 
621: 		if !DefaultIntfRegexp.MatchString(IFaceName) {
622: 			return fmt.Errorf(
623: 				"IOL Node %q has an interface named %q which doesn't match the required pattern. %s",
624: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
625: 				IFaceName,
626: 				IntfHelpMsg,
627: 			)
628: 		}
629: 	}
630: 
631: 	return nil
632: }
633: 
634: func (n *iol) UpdateMgmtIntf(ctx context.Context) error {
635: 	// L2 IOL images default to switchport mode, which rejects IP addresses.
636: 	switchportCmd := ""
637: 	if n.isL2Node {
638: 		switchportCmd = "no switchport\r"
639: 	}
640: 
641: 	waitCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, bootPromptTimeout)
642: 	defer cancel()
643: 
644: 	// 1. Start streaming container logs
645: 	logReader, err := n.Runtime.StreamLogs(waitCtx, n.Cfg.LongName)
646: 	if err != nil {
647: 		log.Warn("Skipping IOL mgmt interface update, cannot stream container logs",
648: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName, "error", err)
649: 		return nil
650: 	}
651: 	defer logReader.Close()
652: 
653: 	// Ticker to periodically send carriage returns (\r) to wake up the serial console
654: 	ticker := time.NewTicker(2 * time.Second)
655: 	defer ticker.Stop()
656: 
657: 	// Scanner to read the container log output line by line
658: 	scanner := bufio.NewScanner(logReader)
659: 
660: 	// Asynchronously send carriage returns since scanner.Scan() blocks
661: 	tickCtx, stopTicker := context.WithCancel(waitCtx)
662: 	defer stopTicker()
663: 	tickerDone := make(chan struct{})
664: 
665: 	go func() {
666: 		defer close(tickerDone)
667: 		for {
668: 			select {
669: 			case <-tickCtx.Done():
670: 				return
671: 			case <-ticker.C:
672: 				_ = n.Runtime.WriteToStdinNoWait(tickCtx, n.Cfg.LongName, []byte("\r"))
673: 			}
674: 		}
675: 	}()
676: 
677: 	bootComplete := false
678: 	for scanner.Scan() {
679: 		// Trim control characters and spaces from the log line
680: 		line := strings.TrimSpace(scanner.Text())
681: 		if line == "" {
682: 			continue
683: 		}
684: 
685: 		log.Debugf("[IOL Boot Log] %s", line)
686: 
687: 		// Match boot prompts (>, #, or initial config dialog)
688: 		if strings.HasSuffix(line, ">") ||
689: 			strings.HasSuffix(line, "#") ||
690: 			strings.Contains(line, "initial configuration dialog?") {
691: 
692: 			// Ignore syslog messages containing '>'
693: 			if strings.Contains(line, "%") {
694: 				continue
695: 			}
696: 
697: 			log.Infof("IOL boot prompt detected (%s). Proceeding with configuration.", line)
698: 			bootComplete = true
699: 			break
700: 		}
701: 	}
702: 
703: 	stopTicker()
704: 	<-tickerDone
705: 
706: 	if !bootComplete {
707: 		if ctx.Err() != nil {
708: 			return ctx.Err()
709: 		}
710: 		if waitCtx.Err() != nil {
711: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q: timed out after %s waiting for IOL boot prompt",
712: 				n.Cfg.ShortName, bootPromptTimeout)
713: 		}
714: 		if err := scanner.Err(); err != nil {
715: 			return fmt.Errorf("error reading IOL log stream: %w", err)
716: 		}
717: 	}
718: 	// --- End of prompt detection ---
719: 
720: 	// All IOS commands applied here are idempotent and safe to re-run.
721: 	mgmt_str := fmt.Sprintf(
722: 		"\rend\renable\rconfig terminal\rinterface %s\r%sip address %s %s\rno ipv6 address\ripv6 address %s/%d\rexit\rip route vrf clab-mgmt 0.0.0.0 0.0.0.0 %s %s\ripv6 route vrf clab-mgmt ::/0 %s %s\rend\rwr\r",
723: 		n.mgmtIntf,
724: 		switchportCmd,
725: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Address,
726: 		clabutils.CIDRToDDN(n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4PrefixLength),
727: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Address,
728: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6PrefixLength,
729: 		n.mgmtIntf,
730: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Gateway,
731: 		n.mgmtIntf,
732: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Gateway,
733: 	)
734: 	data := []byte(mgmt_str)
735: 
736: 	var lastErr error
737: 	for i := range 3 {
738: 		if i > 0 {
739: 			time.Sleep(10 * time.Second)
740: 		}
741: 		lastErr = n.Runtime.WriteToStdinNoWait(ctx, n.Cfg.ContainerID, data)
742: 		if lastErr == nil {
743: 			break
744: 		}
745: 		log.Warnf("UpdateMgmtIntf: attempt %d/3 failed for %s: %v",
746: 			i+1, n.Cfg.ShortName, lastErr)
747: 	}
748: 	return lastErr
749: }
750: 
751: // SaveConfig is used for "clab save" functionality -- it saves the running config to the startup
752: // configuration.
753: func (n *iol) SaveConfig(_ context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {
754: 	p, err := platform.NewPlatform(
755: 		"cisco_iosxe",
756: 		n.Cfg.LongName,
757: 		options.WithAuthNoStrictKey(),
758: 		options.WithAuthUsername(n.Cfg.Credentials.Username),
759: 		options.WithAuthPassword(n.Cfg.Credentials.Password),
760: 	)
761: 	if err != nil {
762: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create platform; error: %+v", err)
763: 	}
764: 
765: 	d, err := p.GetNetworkDriver()
766: 	if err != nil {
767: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to fetch network driver from the platform; error: %+v", err)
768: 	}
769: 
770: 	err = d.Open()
771: 	if err != nil {
772: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open driver; error: %+v", err)
773: 	}
774: 
775: 	defer d.Close()
776: 
777: 	_, err = d.SendCommand("write memory")
778: 	if err != nil {
779: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to send command; error: %+v", err)
780: 	}
781: 
782: 	log.Infof(
783: 		"Successfully copied running configuration to startup configuration file for node: %q\n",
784: 		n.Cfg.ShortName,
785: 	)
786: 	return nil, nil
787: }

You can also use ethX-based interface naming.\"\n\n\tvalidTypes = []string{typeIOL, typeL2}\n)\n\n// Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.\nfunc Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {\n\tgenerateNodeAttributes := clabnodes.NewGenerateNodeAttributes(generateable, generateIfFormat)\n\tplatformAttrs := &clabnodes.PlatformAttrs{\n\t\tScrapliPlatformName: scrapliPlatformName,\n\t\tNapalmPlatformName:  NapalmPlatformName,\n\t}\n\n\tnrea := clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(\n\t\tdefaultCredentials,\n\t\tgenerateNodeAttributes,\n\t\tplatformAttrs,\n\t).WithKindSpecificConfig(kindSpecificConfig)\n\n\tr.Register(kindNames, func() clabnodes.Node {\n\t\treturn new(iol)\n\t}, nrea)\n}\n\n// KindSpecificConfig is the cisco_iol kind-specific config, set as keys on the node definition.\ntype KindSpecificConfig struct {\n\t// PidOffset shifts the auto-assigned IOL PID, e.g. to keep IDs unique across labs.\n\tPidOffset *int `yaml:\"pid-offset,omitempty\" json:\"pid-offset,omitempty\"`\n\t// MgmtIntf is the management interface, e.g. Ethernet0/1. Defaults to Ethernet0/0.\n\tMgmtIntf string `yaml:\"mgmt-intf,omitempty\" json:\"mgmt-intf,omitempty\"`\n\t// BootstrapConfig replaces the default startup configuration with a file, or disables it\n\t// when set to \"none\".\n\tBootstrapConfig string `yaml:\"bootstrap-config,omitempty\" json:\"bootstrap-config,omitempty\"`\n}\n\nvar kindSpecificConfig clabnodes.KindSpecificConfigSpec[KindSpecificConfig]\n\ntype iol struct {\n\tclabnodes.DefaultNode\n\n\tisL2Node          bool\n\tPid               string\n\tnvramFile         string\n\tpartialStartupCfg string\n\tbootCfg           string\n\tinterfaces        []IOLInterface\n\tfirstBoot         bool\n\tbootstrapNone     bool\n\tbootstrapCfgFile  string\n\tmgmtIntf          string\n\tmgmtSlot          int\n\tmgmtPort          int\n\tmgmtLinuxIdx      int\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) kindSpecificCfg() *KindSpecificConfig { return kindSpecificConfig.Of(n.Cfg) }\n\nfunc (n *iol) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {\n\t// Init DefaultNode\n\tn.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)\n\tn.firstBoot = false\n\n\tn.Cfg = cfg\n\tfor _, o := range opts {\n\t\to(n)\n\t}\n\n\tnodeType := strings.ToLower(n.Cfg.NodeType)\n\n\tpid := n.Cfg.Index + 1 // n.Cfg.Index is zero-indexed, PID needs to be >= 1\n\tif off := n.kindSpecificCfg().PidOffset; off != nil {\n\t\tpid += *off\n\t}\n\n\tn.Pid = strconv.Itoa(pid)\n\n\tenv := map[string]string{\n\t\t\"IOL_PID\": n.Pid,\n\t}\n\n\tn.Cfg.Env = clabutils.MergeStringMaps(env, n.Cfg.Env)\n\n\t// check if user submitted node type is valid\n\tswitch nodeType {\n\tcase \"\":\n\t\tn.isL2Node = strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(path.Base(n.Cfg.Image)), \"l2\")\n\tcase typeIOL:\n\t\tn.isL2Node = false\n\tcase typeL2:\n\t\tn.isL2Node = true\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"invalid node type '%s'. Valid types are: %s\",\n\t\t\tn.Cfg.NodeType, strings.Join(validTypes, \", \"))\n\t}\n\n\tif err := n.parseMgmtIntf(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tn.Cfg.Env[\"CLAB_IOL_MGMT_INTF\"] = n.mgmtIntf\n\n\tn.nvramFile = fmt.Sprint(\"nvram_\", fmt.Sprintf(\"%05s\", n.Pid))\n\n\tn.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds,\n\t\t// mount nvram so that config persists\n\t\tfmt.Sprint(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile), \":\", path.Join(iol_workdir, n.nvramFile)),\n\n\t\t// mount launch config\n\t\tfmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, \"boot_config.txt\"), \":/iol/config.txt\"),\n\n\t\t// mount IOYAP and NETMAP for interface mapping\n\t\tfmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, \"iouyap.ini\"), \":/iol/iouyap.ini\"),\n\t\tfmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, \"NETMAP\"), \":/iol/NETMAP\"),\n\t)\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// parseMgmtIntf parses the mgmt-intf kind-specific config key and normalizes it to\n// Ethernet<slot>/<port>;\n// empty means the default Ethernet0/0.\nfunc (n *iol) parseMgmtIntf() error {\n\tif v := n.kindSpecificCfg().MgmtIntf; v != \"\" {\n\t\tcaptureGroups, err := clabutils.GetRegexpCaptureGroups(CapturingIntfRegexp, v)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"invalid mgmt-intf %q: %w\\n%s\", v, err, IntfHelpMsg)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tn.mgmtSlot, _ = strconv.Atoi(captureGroups[\"slot\"])\n\t\tn.mgmtPort, _ = strconv.Atoi(captureGroups[\"port\"])\n\n\t\tif n.mgmtSlot > 9 || n.mgmtPort > 3 {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\"invalid mgmt-intf %q: slot must be 0-9 and port must be 0-3\", v)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tn.mgmtIntf = fmt.Sprintf(\"Ethernet%d/%d\", n.mgmtSlot, n.mgmtPort)\n\tn.mgmtLinuxIdx = n.mgmtSlot*4 + n.mgmtPort\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// swapMgmtIdx swaps linux interface index 0 with the management interface's index,\n// so Ethernet0/0 takes the index a relocated management interface vacated.\nfunc (n *iol) swapMgmtIdx(idx int) int {\n\tswitch idx {\n\tcase 0:\n\t\treturn n.mgmtLinuxIdx\n\tcase n.mgmtLinuxIdx:\n\t\treturn 0\n\t}\n\n\treturn idx\n}\n\n// iolPortForLinuxIdx returns the IOL slot/port backed by linux interface index idx.\nfunc (n *iol) iolPortForLinuxIdx(idx int) (slot, port int) {\n\tmapped := n.swapMgmtIdx(idx)\n\treturn mapped / 4, mapped % 4\n}\n\n// ensureNumSlotsEnv exports CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS when the management interface is\n// relocated, so that its slot exists even when no link lands in it.\nfunc (n *iol) ensureNumSlotsEnv() {\n\tif n.mgmtLinuxIdx == 0 || n.Cfg.Env[\"CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS\"] != \"\" {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tintfIdxRegexp := regexp.MustCompile(`\\d+`)\n\tneeded := n.mgmtSlot + 1\n\n\tfor _, ep := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {\n\t\tx, _ := strconv.Atoi(intfIdxRegexp.FindString(ep.GetIfaceName()))\n\t\tif slot, _ := n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x); slot+1 > needed {\n\t\t\tneeded = slot + 1\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tn.Cfg.Env[\"CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS\"] = strconv.Itoa(needed)\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) PreDeploy(ctx context.Context, params *clabnodes.PreDeployParams) error {\n\tclabutils.CreateDirectory(n.Cfg.LabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)\n\n\tif v := n.kindSpecificCfg().BootstrapConfig; v != \"\" {\n\t\tswitch p := clabutils.ResolvePath(v, params.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir()); {\n\t\tcase strings.EqualFold(v, \"none\"):\n\t\t\tn.bootstrapNone = true\n\t\tcase clabutils.FileExists(p):\n\t\t\tn.bootstrapCfgFile = p\n\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"bootstrap-config file %q does not exist\", p)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t_, err := n.LoadOrGenerateCertificate(params.Cert, params.TopologyName)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tn.ensureNumSlotsEnv()\n\n\treturn n.CreateIOLFiles(ctx)\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {\n\tlog.Infof(\"Running postdeploy actions for Cisco IOL '%s' node\", n.Cfg.ShortName)\n\n\tn.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)\n\n\tif err := n.GenBootConfig(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to generate boot config: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Must update mgmt IP if not first boot, unless the node boots without a baseline config\n\tif !n.firstBoot && !n.bootstrapNone {\n\t\treturn n.UpdateMgmtIntf(ctx)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// Start re-applies the mgmt veth TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime\n// recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.\n// See disableMgmtTxOffload for why IOL cannot function with it enabled.\nfunc (n *iol) Start(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tif err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix\n\t// for them as well, as there is no container mgmt veth to adjust.\n\tif n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tn.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// disableMgmtTxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the host NS veth for the mgmt interface.\n// iouyap bridges IOL interfaces over packet sockets; frames carrying an offloaded\n// (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum reach the IOL process with an incomplete checksum, which its\n// TCP/IP stack drops, breaking TCP/UDP connectivity.\n//\n// Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,\n// and there is no clab-created mgmt veth to adjust.\nfunc (n *iol) disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx context.Context) {\n\tif n.Cfg.NetworkMode == \"host\" {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif n.Runtime.Mgmt().Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tvar peerIfIndex int\n\terr := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.VethPeerIndex(\"eth0\", &peerIfIndex))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Warn(\"Failed to get veth peer index for IOL mgmt interface\",\n\t\t\t\"node\", n.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\t\"error\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif err := clabutils.DisableTxOffloadByIndex(peerIfIndex); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Warn(\"Failed to disable TX checksum offload on IOL mgmt host veth\",\n\t\t\t\"node\", n.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\t\"error\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) CreateIOLFiles(ctx context.Context) error {\n\t// If NVRAM already exists, don't need to create\n\t// otherwise saved configs in NVRAM are overwritten.\n\tif !clabutils.FileExists(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile)) {\n\t\t// create nvram file\n\t\tclabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile), \"\")\n\t\tn.firstBoot = true\n\t}\n\t// create these files so the bind monut doesn't automatically\n\t// make folders.\n\tif bootCfg := path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, \"boot_config.txt\"); Each slot contains max 4 ports\n\t\tslot, port = n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x)\n\n\t\t// append data to write to NETMAP and IOUYAP files\n\t\tiouyapData += fmt.Sprintf(\"[513:%d/%d]\\neth_dev = %s\\n\", slot, port, intf.GetIfaceName())\n\t\tnetmapdata += fmt.Sprintf(\"%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\\n\", n.Pid, slot, port, slot, port)\n\n\t\t// populate template array for config\n\t\tipv4Addr := \"\"\n\t\tipv4Mask := \"\"\n\t\tv4Prefix := intf.GetIPv4Addr()\n\t\tipv6Addr := \"\"\n\n\t\tif v4Prefix.IsValid() {\n\t\t\tipv4Addr = v4Prefix.Addr().String()\n\t\t\tipv4Mask = clabutils.CIDRToDDN(v4Prefix.Bits())\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif a := intf.GetIPv6Addr(); a.IsValid() {\n\t\t\tipv6Addr = a.String()\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tn.interfaces = append(n.interfaces,\n\t\t\tIOLInterface{\n\t\t\t\tIfaceName: intf.GetIfaceName(),\n\t\t\t\tIfaceIdx:  x,\n\t\t\t\tSlot:      slot,\n\t\t\t\tPort:      port,\n\t\t\t\tIPv4Addr:  ipv4Addr,\n\t\t\t\tIPv4Mask:  ipv4Mask,\n\t\t\t\tIPv6Addr:  ipv6Addr,\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\n\t// create IOUYAP and NETMAP file for interface mappings\n\terr := clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, \"iouyap.ini\"), iouyapData)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\terr = clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, \"NETMAP\"), netmapdata)\n\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) GenBootConfig(_ context.Context) error {\n\tn.bootCfg = cfgTemplate\n\n\tswitch {\n\tcase n.bootstrapNone:\n\t\tn.bootCfg = \"{{ .PartialCfg }}\"\n\tcase n.bootstrapCfgFile != \"\":\n\t\tcfg, err := os.ReadFile(n.bootstrapCfgFile)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tn.bootCfg = string(cfg)\n\t}\n\n\tif n.Cfg.StartupConfig != \"\" {\n\t\tcfg, err := os.ReadFile(n.Cfg.StartupConfig)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif clabutils.IsPartialConfigFile(n.Cfg.StartupConfig) {\n\t\t\tn.partialStartupCfg = string(cfg)\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tn.bootCfg = string(cfg)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// create startup config template\n\ttpl := IOLTemplateData{\n\t\tHostname:           n.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\tIsL2Node:           n.isL2Node,\n\t\tMgmtIntf:           n.mgmtIntf,\n\t\tMgmtIPv4Addr:       n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Address,\n\t\tMgmtIPv4SubnetMask: clabutils.CIDRToDDN(n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4PrefixLength),\n\t\tMgmtIPv4GW:         n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Gateway,\n\t\tMgmtIPv6Addr:       n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Address,\n\t\tMgmtIPv6PrefixLen:  n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6PrefixLength,\n\t\tMgmtIPv6GW:         n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Gateway,\n\t\tDataIFaces:         n.interfaces,\n\t}\n\n\t// render the partial startup config so template variables work in partials too\n\tif n.partialStartupCfg != \"\" {\n\t\tpartialTpl, err := template.New(\"clab-iol-partial-config\").Funcs(\n\t\t\tclabutils.CreateFuncs()).Parse(n.partialStartupCfg)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tbuf := new(bytes.Buffer)\n\t\tif err := partialTpl.Execute(buf, tpl); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\ttpl.PartialCfg = buf.String()\n\t}\n\n\tIOLCfgTpl, err := IOLCfgTpl.Execute(buf, tpl)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\treturn clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, \"boot_config.txt\"), buf.String())\n}\n\ntype IOLTemplateData struct {\n\tHostname           string\n\tIsL2Node           bool\n\tMgmtIntf           string\n\tMgmtIPv4Addr       string\n\tMgmtIPv4SubnetMask string\n\tMgmtIPv4GW         string\n\tMgmtIPv6Addr       string\n\tMgmtIPv6PrefixLen  int\n\tMgmtIPv6GW         string\n\tDataIFaces         []IOLInterface\n\tPartialCfg         string\n}\n\n// IOLInterface struct stores mapping info between\n// IOL interface name and linux container interface.\ntype IOLInterface struct {\n\tIfaceName string\n\tIfaceIdx  int\n\tSlot      int\n\tPort      int\n\tIPv4Addr  string\n\tIPv4Mask  string\n\tIPv6Addr  string\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) GetMappedInterfaceName(ifName string) (string, error) {\n\tcaptureGroups, err := clabutils.GetRegexpCaptureGroups(CapturingIntfRegexp, ifName)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\n\tindexGroups := []string{\"slot\", \"port\"}\n\tparsedIndices := make(map[string]int)\n\tfoundIndices := make(map[string]bool)\n\n\tfor _, indexKey := range indexGroups {\n\t\tif index, found := captureGroups[indexKey]; found && index != \"\" {\n\t\t\tfoundIndices[indexKey] = true\n\t\t\tparsedIndices[indexKey], err = strconv.Atoi(index)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn \"\", fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\t\"%q parsed %s index %q could not be cast to an integer\",\n\t\t\t\t\tifName,\n\t\t\t\t\tindexKey,\n\t\t\t\t\tindex,\n\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif parsedIndices[indexKey] < 0 {\n\t\t\t\treturn \"\", fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\t\"%q parsed %q index %q does not match requirement >= 0\",\n\t\t\t\t\tifName,\n\t\t\t\t\tindexKey,\n\t\t\t\t\tindex,\n\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tfoundIndices[indexKey] = false\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// return an ethX interface name. Slots are in 'groups' of 4 interfaces each\n\tif foundIndices[\"slot\"] && foundIndices[\"port\"] {\n\t\tidx := n.swapMgmtIdx((parsedIndices[\"slot\"] * 4) + parsedIndices[\"port\"])\n\t\treturn fmt.Sprintf(\"eth%d\", idx), nil\n\t} else {\n\t\treturn \"\", fmt.Errorf(\"%q missing slot or port index\", ifName)\n\t}\n}\n\n// AddEndpoint override maps the endpoint name to an ethX-based naming where necessary, before\n// adding it to the node endpoints. Returns an error if the mapping goes wrong or if the\n// interface name is NOT allowed.\nfunc (n *iol) AddEndpoint(e clablinks.Endpoint) error {\n\tendpointName := e.GetIfaceName()\n\tvar IFaceName, IFaceAlias string\n\n\tIFaceName = endpointName\n\n\tif !(DefaultIntfRegexp.MatchString(endpointName)) &&\n\t\tAllowedIntfRegexp.MatchString(endpointName) {\n\t\tlog.Debugf(\"%s: %s needs mapping\", n.Cfg.ShortName, endpointName)\n\t\tmappedName, err := n.GetMappedInterfaceName(endpointName)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\"%q interface name %q could not be mapped to an ethX-based interface name: %w\\n%s\",\n\t\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\t\te.GetIfaceName(),\n\t\t\t\terr,\n\t\t\t\tIntfHelpMsg,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t}\n\t\tlog.Debugf(\n\t\t\t\"Interface Mapping: Mapping interface %q (ifAlias) to %q (ifName)\",\n\t\t\tendpointName,\n\t\t\tmappedName,\n\t\t)\n\t\tIFaceName = mappedName\n\t\tIFaceAlias = endpointName\n\t}\n\n\te.SetIfaceName(IFaceName)\n\t// should be nil if ethX naming is used.\n\te.SetIfaceAlias(IFaceAlias)\n\tn.Endpoints = append(n.Endpoints, e)\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) CheckInterfaceName() error {\n\terr := n.CheckInterfaceOverlap()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, e := range n.Endpoints {\n\t\tIFaceName := e.GetIfaceName()\n\t\t// eth0 always backs the management interface\n\t\tif IFaceName == \"eth0\" {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\"IOL Node: %q. Management interface %s (eth0) is not allowed\",\n\t\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\t\tn.mgmtIntf,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif !DefaultIntfRegexp.MatchString(IFaceName) {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\"IOL Node %q has an interface named %q which doesn't match the required pattern. %s\",\n\t\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\t\tIFaceName,\n\t\t\t\tIntfHelpMsg,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) UpdateMgmtIntf(ctx context.Context) error {\n\t// L2 IOL images default to switchport mode, which rejects IP addresses.\n\tswitchportCmd := \"\"\n\tif n.isL2Node {\n\t\tswitchportCmd = \"no switchport\\r\"\n\t}\n\n\twaitCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, bootPromptTimeout)\n\tdefer cancel()\n\n\t// 1. Proceeding with configuration.\", line)\n\t\t\tbootComplete = true\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tstopTicker()\n\t<-tickerDone\n\n\tif !bootComplete {\n\t\tif ctx.Err() != nil {\n\t\t\treturn ctx.Err()\n\t\t}\n\t\tif waitCtx.Err() != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q: timed out after %s waiting for IOL boot prompt\",\n\t\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName, bootPromptTimeout)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := scanner.Err(); 74: ## Interface naming
75: 
76: You can use [interfaces names](../topo-def-file.md#interface-naming) in the topology file like they appear in the -{{ kind_display_name }}- CLI.
77: 
78: The interface naming convention is: `Ethernet0/X` (or `e0/X`), where `X` is the port number.
79: 
80: With the default `Ethernet0/0` management interface:
81: 
82: - `e0/1` - First data-plane interface available
83: - `e0/2` - Second data-plane interface, and so on...
84: 
85: Keep in mind IOL defines interfaces in groups of 4. Every four interfaces (zero-indexed), the slot index increments by one.
86: 
87: - `e0/3` - Third data-plane interface
88: - `e1/0` - Fourth data-plane interface
89: - `e1/1` - Fifth data-plane interface
90: 
91: The example ports above would be mapped to the following Linux interfaces inside the container running -{{ kind_display_name }}-:
92: 
93: - `eth0` - management interface connected to the containerlab management network. Mapped to `Ethernet0/0`.
94: - `eth1` - First data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet0/1` interface.
95: - `eth2` - Second data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet0/2` interface
96: - `eth3` - Third data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet0/3` interface
97: - `eth4` - Fourth data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet1/0` interface
98: - `eth5` - Fifth data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet1/1` interface and so on...
99: 
100: When containerlab launches -{{ kind_display_name }}-, the `Ethernet0/0` interface of the container gets assigned management IPv4 and IPv6 addresses from docker. The `Ethernet0/0` interface is in it's own management VRF so that configuration in the global context will not affect the management interface.
101: 
102: Interfaces can be defined in a non-contiguous manner in your topology file. See the example below.
103: 
104: ```yaml
105: name: my-iol-lab
106: topology:
107:   nodes:
108:     iol-1:
109:       kind: cisco_iol
110:       image: vrnetlab/cisco_iol:17.12.01
111:     iol-2:
112:       kind: cisco_iol
113:       image: vrnetlab/cisco_iol:17.12.01
114: 
115:   links:
116:     - endpoints: ["iol-1:Ethernet0/1","iol-2:Ethernet0/1"] 
117:     - endpoints: ["iol-1:Ethernet1/3", "iol-2:Ethernet1/0"]
118: ```
119: 
120: /// warning
121: When defining interfaces non-contigiously you may see more interfaces than you have defined in the -{{ kind_short_display_name }}- CLI, this is because interfaces are provisioned in groups.
122: 
123: At minimum you will see all numerically-lower indexed interfaces in the CLI compared to the interface you have defined, you may also see interfaces with a higher numerical index.
124: 
125: **Links/interfaces that you did not define in your containerlab topology will *not* pass any traffic.**
126: ///
127: 
128: ### Management interface
129: 
130: By default the containerlab reserves an interface on the router for containerlab management access.
131: 
132: By default and for simplicity, this interface is set to `Ethernet0/0`.
133: 
134: If you intend to use `Ethernet0/0` for links in your topology, the management interface can be changed with the `mgmt-intf` [kind-specific config key](../nodes.md#kind-specific-config).
135: 
136: ```yaml
137: topology:
138:   nodes:
139:     iol1:
140:       kind: cisco_iol
141:       image: ...
142:       mgmt-intf: Ethernet3/3
143: ```
144: 
145: With the example above the management VRF, addressing and default routes move to `Ethernet3/3`. Furthermore links on `Ethernet3/3` are rejected instead of `Ethernet0/0`, and `Ethernet0/0` becomes available to use as a data interface in the `links` section of the topology.
146: 
147: /// note
148: Automatic slot allocation requires an IOL image built with [srl-labs/vrnetlab PR #526](https://github.com/srl-labs/vrnetlab/pull/526) or later.
149: 

(Showing lines 30-149 of 283. Use offset=150 to continue.)
</content>

Reasoning
The doc says "telnet <container-name/id> 5000" and "The console is served on the node's management address". These are contradictory: `telnet <container-name>` from the host will not resolve to the management address unless the host has name resolution. Hmm, actually in Docker, container names are resolvable via DNS only within user-defined networks, not from the host. But containerlab users often have the clab mgmt network as a docker bridge; the host cannot resolve container names. However — wait, is there something in containerlab that adds /etc/hosts entries? I don't think so.

Actually, hold on. Let me check how other vrnetlab kinds document telnet console. E.g., vr-sros docs, sros docs mention telnet console: `telnet <container-name> 5000`? Let me grep docs for "telnet" to see the established pattern.

The new sentence "The console is served on the node's management address, so it stays reachable when the IOS management configuration is broken" — this explains why the console works even if IOS mgmt config broken. Fine; possibly the intent is that users can telnet to the mgmt IP too. Not a defect I'd flag confidently. Actually wait — is `telnet <container-name>` resolvable on host? Docker names are resolvable from within user-defined networks by the embedded DNS; from the host, you can use `docker inspect`... Actually many containerlab docs indeed say `telnet clab-topo-node1 5000`. Hmm, I believe there IS something: containerlab's mgmt network is a user-defined bridge network, and Docker's embedded DNS at 127.0.0.11 only within containers. From the host, no. But users could use `clab inspect` for mgmt addresses. This doc pattern issue is pre-existing across many kinds — not introduced by this PR. Skip.

Actually, wait: for cisco_iol specifically, the new sentence says the console is served on the node's management address. Given other kinds' docs claim the same pattern, consistent. I won't flag.

Hmm, but one nuance: the new doc says console "stays reachable when the IOS management configuration is broken" — that's a benefit claim about IOL specifically (IOS mgmt config broken but the console is at TCP level of the container, which is independent of IOS's internal config). True since the console is vrnetlab's serial-over-telnet, independent of IOS config. Fine.

Now, the main code review. Let me look at the lifecycle: `core/lifecycle.go` lines 40-70 — park/restore during "recreate" flows. When is `ParkEndpoints` called and when is `Deploy` called relative to it? If a node is recreated (destroy+deploy), CLAB_INTFS at Deploy time: `d.Endpoints` — during park, are endpoints moved out of `d.Endpoints`? Looking at `CaptureFrom`: captureCandidates adopts runtime endpoints into src node (AdoptEndpoint appends to src.Endpoints), then moveEndpoint(ep, p) — does moveEndpoint release from src and adopt into p? Let me check moveEndpoint in links package.

Reasoning
Parking is pre-existing at base. This PR makes the IOL/linux/deploy env-var and interface-config generation account for parked endpoints.

Key flows to trace:

1. During a recreate (park → destroy container → deploy → restore):
   - Park moves endpoints from node into parkingNode. Question: does `d.Endpoints` keep entries when parked? Looking at ParkEndpoints: `parkingNode.CaptureFrom(ctx, d)` — captureCandidates: tracked = d.GetEndpoints(); for present ifaces, ep from tracked or new RuntimeEndpoint adopted into src (AdoptEndpoint appends to src.Endpoints). Then moveEndpoint(ep, p) presumably releases from src and adopts into p. So after parking, d.Endpoints loses those endpoints (released). Let me verify moveEndpoint.

   Then the node is recreated: Deploy is called (new container created). At Deploy time, CLAB_INTFS = len(EndpointsWithParked()) = len(d.Endpoints (now empty)) + len(parking endpoints) = total. Good — vrnetlab will wait for all interfaces. Then RestoreEndpoints moves them back.

2. For IOL: PreDeploy (CreateIOLFiles → GenInterfaceConfig) runs before Deploy? Order: PreDeploy runs for all nodes, then Deploy. At PreDeploy time after parking, EndpointsWithParked includes parked endpoints → NETMAP/iouyap.ini include mappings for all interfaces. Good.

   But wait — the timing: when recreating, park happens before the container destroy. At that point, is the same node instance reused with its Endpoints? Yes, c.Nodes persist in the process.

3. Question about `EndpointsWithParked` when `parkingNode` exists but is empty: After RestoreEndpoints succeeds, d.parkingNode = nil (line 1242). OK.

Now the concerns:

A. In `Deploy` (default_node.go line 194) and linux.go, env var set at create. But is `Config()` env map shared/used elsewhere? Fine.

B. Does `sros` kind need the same treatment? sros.go line 1838-1852 overrides Park/Restore. sros has its own Deploy env handling maybe. The PR only touched default_node and linux. Is there another node kind that sets CLAB_INTFS? grep for ClabEnvIntfs.

Reasoning
Let me see the rest of moveEndpoint and also the DefaultNode's Deploy flow to see when CLAB_INTFS is set relative to ParkEndpoints. Actually more importantly: what happens on plain `clab deploy` for a lab where the node container already exists? Or on `clab restart`? The env var is set at create-time only.

Now, potential issue: after ParkEndpoints, `d.Endpoints` is emptied (moved to parkingNode). During recreate, Deploy is called; EndpointsWithParked counts parked endpoints — good. But then RestoreEndpoints moves them back and sets d.parkingNode = nil. Fine.

But what about the `iol` node kind's `CheckInterfaceName` and other code that iterates `n.Endpoints`? Not changed.

Now the IOL-specific concern: `GenInterfaceConfig` iterates EndpointsWithParked. Parked endpoints at PreDeploy time (recreate flow). But there's a subtlety: the parked endpoints were moved into the parking netns; their `GetIfaceName` remains e.g. "eth1". GenInterfaceConfig maps eth1 → slot/port. That's the desired preservation. Good.

Another subtlety: in `GenInterfaceConfig`, for a parked endpoint the interface is NOT in the container when IOL boots; iouyap will try to bind eth_dev = eth1 which doesn't exist yet — but it's also the case right after deploy before RestoreEndpoints. iouyap presumably retries or fails until the interface appears. This is the point of the feature: NETMAP must include parked interfaces so that when restored, mapping is right. OK.

Now `ensureNumSlotsEnv`: called in PreDeploy. With parked endpoints included, needed slots computed correctly.

Now, is there an ordering issue: `PreDeploy` for IOL calls `ensureNumSlotsEnv` then `CreateIOLFiles` → `GenInterfaceConfig`. Both use EndpointsWithParked. But does parking happen before PreDeploy in the recreate flow? Let me check the apply/reconcile flow order in core/topology_reconcile.go — where parkRecreatedNodes is called relative to deploy. Let me grep in core for parkRecreatedNodes call sites.

Reasoning
Good: park → delete nodes → deploy nodes → restore. So at `Deploy` time (deployApplyNodes), the node has parked endpoints (d.Endpoints empty, parkingNode set) → CLAB_INTFS counts parked. Good.

Now, one important check: in the classic `clab destroy --all && clab deploy` path (separate processes), the parked state doesn't persist; endpoints are in d.Endpoints after fresh load. Fine.

But what about `clab redeploy` (nodes/redeploy command)? Let me check how redeploy works — does it use the apply flow? Probably. Not needed.

Now consider: does `deployApplyNodes` call `node.Deploy` — for IOL, `Deploy` is inherited from DefaultNode? iol doesn't override Deploy. DefaultNode.Deploy sets CLAB_INTFS via EndpointsWithParked. Good.

But wait: there's an ordering subtlety for IOL: `PreDeploy` (which generates NETMAP/iouyap.ini) runs inside deployApplyNodes? Let me check core/deploy.go line 791 context — PreDeploy is called per node during deploy. And deployApplyNodes presumably calls the full per-node deploy sequence: PreDeploy → Deploy → ... So at PreDeploy, parked endpoints exist. Good.

Now, another angle: what happens on the initial deploy of a lab where nothing is parked? EndpointsWithParked returns d.Endpoints (unchanged behavior). Good.

Now the concern about `EndpointsWithParked` when parkingNode is non-nil but its GetEndpoints is empty — returns clone; harmless.

Race: Deploy is called concurrently? deployApplyNodes with maxWorkers — but Deploy for a given node runs once. The append(slices.Clone...) avoids mutating d.Endpoints — good, this is why they used Clone. But wait: `append(slices.Clone(d.Endpoints), ...)` — slices.Clone returns a new slice with len=cap possibly? slices.Clone returns a slice with the same length; appending will allocate. Fine.

Hmm, but actually there's a subtle bug: `EndpointsWithParked` returns `d.Endpoints` directly when parkingNode == nil — callers like `len()` fine. OK.

Now the more serious question: does including parked endpoints in `GenInterfaceConfig` break the non-parked case? No.

Next: check `iol` type's `AddEndpoint` — parked endpoints captured by parking node were previously added via AddEndpoint (names mapped to ethX). The endpoint keeps GetIfaceName as ethX. Fine.

Now — an important potential correctness gap: the sros kind overrides ParkEndpoints/RestoreEndpoints but sets CLAB_INTFS? sros is a vr node; vr_node.go maybe sets CLAB_INTFS elsewhere? grep showed only linux.go and default_node.go set ClabEnvIntfs. sros inherits DefaultNode.Deploy presumably. OK.

Now check `nodes/linux/linux.go` — linux kind overrides Deploy; it calls EndpointsWithParked. But note: does linux node's Deploy get called with parked endpoints? linux is a DefaultNode subclass, so parking works. Good.

Now — what about `GetEndpoints()` interface method: nodes.Node interface has GetEndpoints; the new method `EndpointsWithParked` is only on DefaultNode, so other node kinds (e.g., remote kinds like ceos, srl which embed DefaultNode) get it too. sros wraps? sros embeds DefaultNode too presumably.

Now think about correctness issue: in DefaultNode.Deploy, CLAB_INTFS is set BEFORE CreateContainer. But when the deploy happens for a *recreated* node, parked endpoints count. However — is CLAB_INTFS supposed to count only data interfaces (endpoints)? Yes: "number of interfaces (endpoints) a node has... doesn't count eth0". Parked endpoints are still endpoints of the node (will be restored). Including them is correct so vrnetlab waits.

Edge: what if restore fails and node remains parked? Then CLAB_INTFS counted them. Fine.

Now, another potential issue with `EndpointsWithParked` in `GenInterfaceConfig`: parked endpoints include RuntimeEndpoints discovered in the parking netns (DiscoverOwnedEndpoints creates NewRuntimeEndpoint for interfaces present in parking ns that aren't tracked). Those have iface names from netns. Fine.

Now let's check the second hunk in iol.go: boot_config.txt creation guard. Behavior change: previously PreDeploy truncated boot_config.txt unconditionally; now it leaves existing content. Then PostDeploy GenBootConfig always rewrites. So the only case where content differs: PostDeploy fails or isn't reached (e.g., deploy error between PreDeploy and PostDeploy), or `PostDeploy` skipped (`--skip-post-deploy`? there is skipPostDeploy in apply options!). In `clab deploy --skip-post-deploy`... hmm wait, but PreDeploy also runs? If a node is being recreated with skipPostDeploy, previously boot_config.txt was truncated (IOL would boot with empty config → unconfigured); now the old boot config persists (IOL boots with last generated config). Which is intended? The change preserves saved config. This looks like a deliberate fix: "so that saved configs are overwritten" comment for nvram; boot_config.txt same treatment. Makes sense.

But wait — there's a subtle issue: if the user changes `startup-config` in the topology and redeploys, GenBootConfig overwrites boot_config.txt anyway. OK.

However, there's a real difference: the old code created/truncated boot_config.txt in PreDeploy to satisfy the bind mount (file must exist before container create). If the file doesn't exist, docker creates a *directory* for a bind-mount path that doesn't exist — hence the comment. New code only creates if missing, so bind still fine.

Hmm — but wait, there's a subtle path issue: what if lab dir is recreated fresh (`clab deploy --reconfigure`)? LabDir gets wiped? In reconfigure, the lab dir may be removed, so boot_config.txt missing → created. Fine.

Now the docs change correctness: "The console is served on the node's management address, so it stays reachable when the IOS management configuration is broken." Then `telnet <container-name/id> 5000` — if the console is served on the management address, telnet to container name from host... The host can't resolve the container name to the mgmt IP (no DNS). Unless the user runs it from a container on the same network. Established docs pattern for other kinds says "# from container host / telnet <container-name> 5000" — hmm, so how does that resolve? Let me think about how vrnetlab exposes console: it binds 0.0.0.0:5000 in the container. From the host, you'd need the container IP. Container names aren't resolvable... unless the user has configured /etc/hosts or uses docker's DNS. Actually wait — maybe containerlab's management network setup adds the host as able to resolve? No.

Hmm, but many containerlab users run `telnet clab-x-node1 5000` successfully? I believe there's a helper: containerlab doesn't add host entries. Actually, I recall that containerlab docs use this pattern and it works because... hmm, I'm not sure. It's out of scope — pattern is pre-existing across many kinds; the PR merely follows it. I won't flag it, or maybe mention in coverage notes.

Now let me look for something more substantive: check the base versions of the changed files to diff precisely, especially default_node.go Deploy context and whether there are tests covering EndpointsWithParked. Also check `iol_test.go` in base/head for GenInterfaceConfig tests.

173: 				d.Cfg.ShortName,
174: 			)
175: 		}
176: 		err := d.Runtime.PullImage(ctx, imageName, d.Config().ImagePullPolicy)
177: 		if err != nil {
178: 			return err
179: 		}
180: 	}
181: 	return nil
182: }

184: func (d *DefaultNode) VerifyHostRequirements() error {
185: 	return d.HostRequirements.Verify(d.Cfg.Kind, d.Cfg.ShortName)
186: }
187: 
188: func (d *DefaultNode) Deploy(ctx context.Context, _ *DeployParams) error {
189: 	// Set the "CLAB_INTFS" variable to the number of interfaces (endpoints) a node has.
190: 	// This env var does not count in the eth0 interface that is automatically created by the
191: 	// container runtime. This env var is used by some containers (e.g. vrnetlab systems) to
192: 	// postpone the startup until all interfaces
193: 	// have been added to the container namespace.
194: 	d.Config().Env[clabconstants.ClabEnvIntfs] = strconv.Itoa(len(d.EndpointsWithParked()))
195: 
196: 	// create the container
197: 	cID, err := d.Runtime.CreateContainer(ctx, d.Cfg)
198: 	if err != nil {
199: 		return err
200: 	}
201: 
202: 	// start the container
203: 	_, err = d.Runtime.StartContainer(ctx, cID, d)
204: 	if err != nil {
205: 		return err
206: 	}
207: 
208: 	// Update the nodes state
209: 	d.SetState(clabnodesstate.Deployed)
210: 
211: 	return nil
212: }
213: 
214: // GetNSPath retrieves the nodes nspath.
215: func (d *DefaultNode) GetNSPath(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
216: 	var err error
217: 	nsp := ""
218: 
219: 	if d.Cfg.IsRootNamespaceBased {
220: 		netns, err := ns.GetCurrentNS()
221: 		if err != nil {
222: 			return "", err
223: 		}
224: 		nsp = netns.Path()
225: 	}
226: 	if nsp == "" {
227: 		nsp, err = d.Runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, d.OverwriteNode.GetContainerName())
228: 		if err != nil {
229: 			log.Errorf("Unable to determine NetNS Path for node %s: %v", d.Cfg.ShortName, err)
230: 			return "", err
231: 		}
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	return nsp, err
235: }

Reasoning
Now let me consider the ComputeDiff angle: NodeConfig comparison — when a node is recreated with parked endpoints, the CLAB_INTFS env value may differ from the persisted state env (e.g., previously CLAB_INTFS=3 when not parked; during recreate Deploy sets it again, same). Actually CLAB_INTFS is set in Deploy always before create, so env value depends on current endpoint state — but diffing compares oldCfg (from lab state file) vs newCfg (computed from topology). The newCfg env computed at... config load time won't include CLAB_INTFS (set during Deploy). Hmm, where is CLAB_INTFS persisted? NodeConfig.Env is written to the lab state/ansible inventory. If during recreate the endpoints count is the same, no diff. But consider: `clab links apply` adding a link to a non-recreated node → no env change needed. For recreated nodes, env is set again. But ComputeDiff compares old vs new NodeConfig where new NodeConfig.Env is built from the topology file without CLAB_INTFS; old has CLAB_INTFS. Would that trigger a spurious "Env" diff for every node? This is pre-existing behavior (base also sets CLAB_INTFS in Deploy). Let me check where oldCfg comes from — maybe loaded state excludes runtime-set envs. Actually, hmm: the newCfg used in diff is built during config load; oldCfg is from the saved lab state after previous deploy (which included CLAB_INTFS? The state file saves node configs post-deploy...). If that were an issue, it'd be pre-existing. Skip.

Now the important thing to check: `EndpointsWithParked` counts parked endpoints in `Deploy`, but what about nodes whose endpoints get parked and then the container is NOT recreated (restartApplyNodes, stop/start)? Not relevant.

Reasoning
Important flow insight: `Stop` calls `ParkEndpoints` (pre-existing at base too). After a `clab stop`, the node is stopped and its endpoints parked; `d.Endpoints` is now empty and `d.parkingNode` set (persisted only in memory; on a new process, RestoreEndpoints reconstructs from netns).

Now the critical scenario for this PR: after `clab stop`, the user runs `clab deploy` again (new process). Node loading: when loading an existing lab, does the node get parked endpoints? No — in a fresh process, `d.parkingNode` is nil. So `Deploy` sets CLAB_INTFS = len(d.Endpoints). But where does d.Endpoints get populated in a new process? From topology links (ResolveLinks). So count is right.

BUT: what about `GenInterfaceConfig` in PreDeploy of a *new process* after a stop? d.Endpoints from topology — all endpoints present. Fine.

Now the in-process recreate flow: park → delete nodes → deploy nodes → restore. During deploy of IOL: PreDeploy → CreateIOLFiles → GenInterfaceConfig with EndpointsWithParked (includes parked endpoints since d.Endpoints is now empty but parkingNode holds them). CLAB_INTFS in Deploy counts them.

Wait — but there's a subtlety: in the recreate flow, does `deployApplyNodes` → node Deploy call happen with the node's `parkingNode` set? Yes, ParkEndpoints was called at line 211 before deleteApplyNodes.

But hold on — for IOL recreate: PreDeploy runs CreateIOLFiles → nvram check. In a recreate (delete+create container), LabDir persists. `firstBoot` false → PostDeploy runs UpdateMgmtIntf, which waits for boot prompt and reconfigures mgmt over console via stdin. OK.

Now — question: is there an issue where `RestoreTo` moves endpoints back into `d` and then `deployLinks` runs (line 231) which re-adds endpoints to node's Endpoints? Wait: after RestoreEndpoints, the endpoints are back in d.Endpoints (restored via AdoptEndpoint). Then `deployLinks(ctx, plan.addedLinks...)` — only added links. Hmm, but the restored endpoints — were they "deployed" (veth created and connected)? The interfaces were moved from parking ns back into the container ns. Fine.

Wait, actually there's something important: in the recreate flow, restoreRecreatedNodes happens after deployApplyNodes but before deployLinks. The restored endpoints are moved into the container's netns directly (moveEndpoint into dst node's netns). OK.

Now — the key question about correctness of this PR's changes: Are there other places that count endpoints at container-creation time that now need parked inclusion? E.g., ceos? srl? srl sets its own env vars? Let me check `nodes/srl` for CLAB_INTFS-like counting. Grep for "INTFS" done — only the two. OK.

Another: `nodes/vr_node.go` Deploy? Let me check whether vr_node Deploy (used by vr-*) inherits DefaultNode.Deploy. vr_node is a separate struct `VrNode` maybe embedding DefaultNode. Let me check.

13: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
