15: 	clabnodes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/nodes"
16: 	clabnodesstate "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/nodes/state"
17: 	clabtypes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types"
18: 	clabutils "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/utils"
19: )
20: 
21: const (
22: 	generateable     = true
23: 	generateIfFormat = "eth%d"
24: )
25: 
26: var kindnames = []string{"linux"}
27: 
28: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
29: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
30: 	generateNodeAttributes := clabnodes.NewGenerateNodeAttributes(generateable, generateIfFormat)
31: 	nrea := clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(nil, generateNodeAttributes, nil)
32: 
33: 	r.Register(kindnames, func() clabnodes.Node {
34: 		return new(linux)
35: 	}, nrea)
36: }
37: 
38: type linux struct {
39: 	clabnodes.DefaultNode
40: }
41: 
42: func (*linux) LinkApplyMode(context.Context) clabnodes.LinkApplyMode {
43: 	return clabnodes.LinkApplyModeLive
44: }
45: 
46: func (n *linux) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
47: 	// Init DefaultNode
48: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)
49: 	n.Cfg = cfg
50: 
51: 	n.StopSignal = clabtypes.SIGKILL
52: 
53: 	// linux kind uses `always` as a default restart policy
54: 	// since often they run auxiliary services that might fail because
55: 	// of the wrong configuration or other reasons.
56: 	// Usually we want those services to automatically restart.
57: 	if n.Cfg.RestartPolicy == "" {
58: 		n.Cfg.RestartPolicy = "always"
59: 	}
60: 
61: 	for _, o := range opts {
62: 		o(n)
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	// make ipv6 enabled on all linux node interfaces
66: 	// but not for the nodes with host network mode, as this is not supported on gh action runners
67: 	if cfg.Sysctls != nil && n.Config().NetworkMode != "host" {
68: 		cfg.Sysctls["net.ipv6.conf.all.disable_ipv6"] = "0"
69: 	}
70: 
71: 	return nil
72: }
73: 
74: func (n *linux) Deploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.DeployParams) error {
75: 	// Set the "CLAB_INTFS" variable to the number of interfaces
76: 	// Which is required by vrnetlab to determine if all configured interfaces are present
77: 	// such that the internal VM can be started with these interfaces assigned.
78: 	n.Config().Env[clabconstants.ClabEnvIntfs] = strconv.Itoa(len(n.EndpointsWithParked()))
79: 
80: 	cID, err := n.Runtime.CreateContainer(ctx, n.Cfg)
81: 	if err != nil {
82: 		return err
83: 	}
84: 	_, err = n.Runtime.StartContainer(ctx, cID, n)
85: 
86: 	n.SetState(clabnodesstate.Deployed)
87: 
88: 	return err
89: }
90: 
91: func (n *linux) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
92: 	log.Debugf("Running postdeploy actions for Linux '%s' node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
93: 
94: 	n.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)
95: 
96: 	return nil
97: }
98: 
99: // Start re-applies the eth0 TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime recreates
100: // the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
101: // See disableEth0TxOffload for why TX offload must stay disabled on eth0.
102: func (n *linux) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
103: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
104: 		return err
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix
108: 	// for them as well, as there is no container netns to adjust.
109: 	if n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
110: 		return nil
111: 	}
112: 
113: 	n.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)
114: 
115: 	return nil
116: }
117: 
118: // disableEth0TxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the container's eth0 interface.
119: // Frames crossing the veth with an offloaded (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum break consumers
120: // that bridge or capture traffic on that interface, so the setting is (re-)applied on
121: // deploy and after every stop/start cycle, when the runtime recreates the mgmt veth.
122: //
123: // Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,
124: // and toggling its offload settings would alter the host networking state.
125: func (n *linux) disableEth0TxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
126: 	if n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "host" {
127: 		return
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.NSEthtoolTXOff(n.GetShortName(), "eth0"))
131: 	if err != nil {
132: 		log.Error(err)
133: 	}
134: }
135: 
136: func (n *linux) GetImages(_ context.Context) map[string]string {
137: 	images := make(map[string]string)
138: 	images[clabnodes.ImageKey] = n.Cfg.Image
139: 
140: 	return images
141: }
142: 
143: // CheckInterfaceName allows any interface name for linux nodes, but checks
144: // if eth0 is only used with network-mode=none.
145: func (n *linux) CheckInterfaceName() error {
146: 	nm := strings.ToLower(n.Cfg.NetworkMode)
147: 	for _, e := range n.Endpoints {
148: 		if e.GetIfaceName() == "eth0" && nm != "none" {
149: 			return fmt.Errorf(
150: 				"eth0 interface name is not allowed for %s node when network mode is not set to none",
151: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
152: 			)
153: 		}
154: 	}
155: 	return nil
156: }

Wait — linux.Deploy duplicates DefaultNode.Deploy's CLAB_INTFS logic (pre-existing duplication: both set the env var with a similar comment). The PR updated both call sites instead of just default_node (linux overrides Deploy anyway). That's fine.

Hmm — but hold on: the linux kind's Deploy sets CLAB_INTFS. Does the linux kind even need it? Pre-existing. OK.

Now: does linux kind use EndpointsWithParked — linux is a DefaultNode embedder; does linux support parking? DefaultNode.ParkEndpoints is generic; Stop/Start overridden by linux to call DefaultNode's. So yes.

Now think about whether there's an actual bug here.

Potential issue A: `EndpointsWithParked` double-counts after a *failed* restore that returns endpoints partially? If RestoreTo fails mid-way, it rolls back moved endpoints back to parking. But `DiscoverOwnedEndpoints` at the start of RestoreTo can AdoptEndpoint into `original` (dst) endpoints — line 125-128: for endpoints in p.GetEndpoints() that are no longer discovered in the parking netns, it moves ownership back to `original` (AdoptEndpoint into d.Endpoints) even though the interface isn't moved into the container netns yet! Wait — those endpoints were "released" from the parking ns (not present anymore). Hmm, when would a parked endpoint disappear from the parking netns? If the container start (Start) recreated netns? Actually in `Start`, RestoreEndpoints runs after StartContainer. Endpoints are in parking netns. RestoreTo → DiscoverOwnedEndpoints lists ifaces in parking netns; tracked = original.GetEndpoints() ∪ p.GetEndpoints(). All parked ifaces should be present. If one is missing (e.g. deleted), it's re-adopted by original. Then endpoints := p.GetEndpoints() — remaining ones get moved. The re-adopted one is not moved (already gone). Then d.Endpoints has it but it doesn't exist anywhere. Edge case, pre-existing.

Now: in this state, `EndpointsWithParked` = d.Endpoints (includes re-adopted) + p.GetEndpoints() — fine, no double count since ownership is exclusive.

But wait — is ownership exclusive at the time `Deploy` runs in the recreate flow? ParkEndpoints: CaptureFrom → moveEndpoint moves ownership to p exclusively (src.ReleaseEndpoint). So d.Endpoints loses them. Then EndpointsWithParked = p.GetEndpoints() only. Good.

However — one real concern: `RestoreEndpoints` failure path. If restore fails during `restoreRecreatedNodes` (core/apply.go line 223), the error propagates and the apply fails, but endpoints remain parked. Then the user re-runs apply: `planApply` — the node exists (running), so not recreated; the parked endpoints... `ReconcileEndpoints`? There's core/topology_reconcile.go with `applyParkingLinkNode`. Complex. Not this PR.

Potential issue B: `EndpointsWithParked` when parkingNode is set but RestoreEndpoints has already restored (parkingNode nil) — fine.

Potential issue C — the actual subtle one: In the recreate flow, `deployApplyNodes` calls `node.Deploy`. For DefaultNode.Deploy, CLAB_INTFS counts parked endpoints. But wait — the endpoints are parked in the parking netns and will be moved to the container netns AFTER StartContainer. vrnetlab waits for CLAB_INTFS interfaces before starting the VM. Correct.

But for IOL: PreDeploy runs before Deploy within deployApplyNodes? Let me verify the per-node deploy sequence in core/deploy.go around line 791.

Reasoning
Order confirmed: per node, PreDeploy → Deploy. Parked endpoints included in both. Good.

Now, one more important check on `GenInterfaceConfig` when the IOL node is recreated with parked endpoints: `n.interfaces` accumulates into the template data for boot config. In the recreate flow, the node instance is the same as before (loaded in this process). Was `n.interfaces` populated already during the initial deploy? In the same process, if the node was deployed earlier (initial deploy), `n.interfaces` has entries; then a `clab links apply` triggers recreate → PreDeploy again → GenInterfaceConfig appends again → duplicates in the boot config template! Wait, was this pre-existing? In base, GenInterfaceConfig also appended without clearing, and PreDeploy is called again on recreate in base too (if the base had a recreate flow — yes, apply existed at base with recreate). Hmm, but base had the same accumulate issue for normal redeploys in-process. Actually wait — is deploy in the same process for the initial deploy and the apply? `clab links apply` is a separate CLI invocation from `clab deploy`, so fresh process → n.interfaces empty. And within one process, deployNode is called once per node. So no accumulation in practice. Fine, not a real issue.

Now the more interesting check: IOL's parked endpoint handling in `GenInterfaceConfig` — parked endpoints' IPv4/IPv6 addresses: `intf.GetIPv4Addr()` — parked endpoints (moved from container) retain their configured prefix? EndpointGeneric has IPv4Addr/IPv6Addr fields set at config load. Since ownership moved to parkingNode but the endpoint object itself persists, GetIPv4Addr still returns the configured values. So the boot config includes correct addresses. Good — actually this is the point: on recreate, the IOL boot config includes data interface IPs.

Hmm wait, but there's a subtlety: `UpdateMgmtIntf` for non-firstBoot reconfigures only the mgmt interface. The data interface IPs in boot config template get applied by IOS at boot (via the generated config). OK.

Now — one thing I should verify: does `RestoreTo` → `ep.Activate(ctx)` set the IP addresses on the restored interface? Yes probably (Activate configures addresses). Fine.

Next, verify the docs claim about Enter key: "After the node boots, press Enter first: IOS ignores console input until it receives a lone Enter." Consistent with UpdateMgmtIntf ticker sending "\r". Fine.

Now, the docs warning: "requires an -{{ kind_short_display_name }}- image built with srl-labs/vrnetlab after November 2026" — today is Nov 2, 2026. This references an external repo change. It's a user-facing doc addition; is it accurate? Can't verify externally. Coverage note.

Now let me check the schema check (check 5): no schema-relevant changes (env var computed internally; doc-only). The `mgmt-intf` and other keys unchanged. Fine.

Now, let me double-check `EndpointsWithParked` for a subtle bug: when `parkingNode != nil` but the parking node's endpoints were *restored partially*... covered.

Another subtle one: `EndpointsWithParked` is defined on `*DefaultNode` — but `iol` embeds DefaultNode by value (`clabnodes.DefaultNode` field, not pointer): `n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)`. So `n.EndpointsWithParked()` works on the embedded copy — d.parkingNode set via `d.parkingNode = parkingNode` where d is the embedded DefaultNode inside iol... wait, careful: DefaultNode methods called on the embedded value mutate the embedded copy, which is fine since it's the same struct embedded in iol. Yes: `n.DefaultNode` is a value field; calling `n.ParkEndpoints(...)` promotes to (&n.DefaultNode).ParkEndpoints, mutating the embedded field. OK.

But — VRNode: `vr.DefaultNode = *NewDefaultNode(n)` same pattern. OK.

Now the concurrency angle: `parkRecreatedNodes` and `restoreRecreatedNodes` iterate sequentially; deployApplyNodes uses maxWorkers (concurrent per-node deploy). `EndpointsWithParked` reads `d.parkingNode` — written in ParkEndpoints (sequential before deploy) and RestoreEndpoints (sequential after deploy). So no race within the apply flow. But is there any concurrent deploy path where Deploy runs while Park/Restore mutate? No.

Now let's check `nodes/default_node_test.go` head around line 380-480 to see the restore ownership test — pre-existing. Any new tests? The diff shows no test changes. Per ponytail rule: "non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind". `EndpointsWithParked` is small; but its integration into CLAB_INTFS count and IOL NETMAP is behavior. The repo has existing tests for park/restore. A small unit test for EndpointsWithParked (nil → same slice; non-nil → clone + parked) would be cheap. P4.

Now let me look more carefully for a real correctness bug in the changed lines.

Change 1: default_node.go Deploy CLAB_INTFS with parked. What about `PostDeploy`/other places that set env? Fine.

Wait — there's one more potential problem: In the recreate flow, when the container is recreated, `UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo` runs and stores the node config (including CLAB_INTFS with parked count) into the lab state. If the restore then fails and the user later runs `clab deploy` in a fresh process, endpoints come from topology → same count. Fine.

But here's a subtle scenario: `clab links apply` where a link is *removed* from a recreated IOL node. Park captures the interfaces present in the netns plus tracked. Then deploy with fewer endpoints → CLAB_INTFS counts EndpointsWithParked — but the parked set includes the interface of the link that was removed! Hmm: plan.staleEndpoints — deleteApplyEndpoints removes stale endpoints before parking. Let me check ordering: `deleteApplyEndpoints(ctx, plan.staleEndpoints)` runs BEFORE parkRecreatedNodes (line 207 before 211). Stale endpoints are removed (Remove deletes the interface from the node's netns and releases from node). So the removed link's endpoint is gone from d.Endpoints before parking. Good.

But what about the interface still present in the container netns but untracked? captureCandidates only captures present ifaces that are tracked or have ownership altname (isOwnedInterface requires known name or altname). Untracked interfaces without altname are left in the container... but the container is destroyed. OK.

Now — deeper issue: in the recreate flow for IOL, `GenInterfaceConfig` runs at PreDeploy with parked endpoints — producing NETMAP entries for parked interfaces. Then after Deploy, restore moves interfaces back. But note: `GenInterfaceConfig` also feeds `n.interfaces` used by GenBootConfig in PostDeploy — includes parked interfaces with their IPs. Good, preserves config.

But wait — PostDeploy for IOL with non-firstBoot calls UpdateMgmtIntf (waits for boot prompt). The boot prompt wait happens BEFORE restoreRecreatedNodes (which happens after deployApplyNodes... actually postDeployApplyNodes runs after deployLinks and restoreRecreatedNodes — order: deployApplyNodes (which calls deployNode: PreDeploy, Deploy, UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo) → restoreRecreatedNodes → deployLinks → postDeployApplyNodes). So PostDeploy (UpdateMgmtIntf) runs after restore. Good: interfaces are back in the container netns by the time IOL boots? Wait — no! The container starts (StartContainer in Deploy) before restore! IOL boots while interfaces are still parked. CLAB_INTFS counts parked endpoints so vrnetlab waits until all interfaces present before starting the VM — that's exactly what this PR fixes. Before this PR, CLAB_INTFS would be 0 (or fewer) and IOL would boot with missing interfaces and wrong CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS. That's the motivation.

Hmm, but wait: even with CLAB_INTFS correct, restore happens after the container starts; vrnetlab waits up to some timeout for interfaces. Restore happens quickly after start. OK.

Now — `ensureNumSlotsEnv` at PreDeploy counts parked endpoints for CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS. Good.

Now consider a *fresh deploy in a new process* of an IOL node whose interfaces were parked (clab stop then clab deploy). In the new process, parkingNode is nil and d.Endpoints contains all endpoints from topology. CLAB_INTFS correct. But — the interfaces are in the parking netns... when the new process deploys the node, does the deploy path handle the existing parking netns? The container was stopped; the parking netns persists (named netns via CreateOrGetNamedNetNS with symlink repointed). On fresh deploy, the node's endpoints (from topology) get deployed via DeployEndpoints → ep.Deploy creates new veths? But the interfaces still exist in the parking netns! Hmm — this is where `restoreRecreatedNodes` vs fresh-process handling matters. Let me check how deploy handles a stopped node with a parking netns in a fresh process. Search core for parkingNetNSPath / ParkingNetnsName usage.

Reasoning
This is a large pre-existing parking subsystem. This PR is a focused follow-up: count parked endpoints for CLAB_INTFS and IOL NETMAP/slot calc, plus doc and boot_config.txt guard.

Now let me hunt for actual defects in the changed code.

### Defect candidate 1: `EndpointsWithParked` aliasing when `parkingNode` non-nil but callers may mutate

Callers: `len()` — fine. iol loops read-only. OK.

### Defect candidate 2: In the recreate flow, does `PreDeploy`/`Deploy` for IOL include parked endpoints *and* the normal `d.Endpoints` without duplication?

After ParkEndpoints: `d.Endpoints` = [] (all moved). So no duplication.

But wait — subtle: `ParkEndpoints` is also called by `Stop`. After `clab stop`, in the SAME process, `clab start` → Start → RestoreEndpoints → parkingNode nil. Fine.

What about `clab restart`? restartApplyNodes uses Stop/Start → park then restore. Fine.

### Defect candidate 3: Double counting when parkingNode retains endpoints AND `d.Endpoints` still contains some of the same interfaces.

Consider `RestoreTo` failure: it rolls back moved endpoints to p — but does `moveEndpoint(ctx, moved[i], p)` re-adopt into p and release from d? Yes. But `ep.Activate` failure path: moves ep back to p — releases from d. OK.

Consider ParkEndpoints reused-parkingNode path (line 1183-1187): reuse parkingNode from failed restore. CaptureFrom → captureCandidates: tracked = d.GetEndpoints() (the node's current endpoints). For interfaces present in the *node's* netns... wait, at this point the container is being stopped; the node netns is the container netns. Hmm, CaptureFrom lists interfaces in src's netns. But if reused parkingNode still holds endpoints (interfaces in parking netns), those aren't in src's netns, so they're not in candidates; they remain in p. And the ones present in src get moved to p. So p.GetEndpoints() = union. No double ownership. But wait: moveEndpoint(dst=p) checks `owned.GetIfaceName() == e.GetIfaceName()` — if p already has an endpoint with the same iface name (from previous park), and src (d) also tracks an endpoint with the same name (because RestoreEndpoints' DiscoverOwnedEndpoints re-adopted a missing iface back into original, line 125-128 — but only when the iface was NOT discovered in the parking netns, meaning it doesn't exist in p's netns)... hmm, edge: iface missing from parking ns (deleted externally) and re-adopted by original; then new park: the iface isn't in src netns either (it was deleted), so not a candidate. No conflict.

OK, double counting seems guarded by exclusive ownership.

### Defect candidate 4: `iol` `EndpointsWithParked` includes *runtime-discovered* parked endpoints in NETMAP — but that's fine.

### Defect candidate 5: `ensureNumSlotsEnv` early-return condition `n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] != ""` — if user set it explicitly, skip. If a previous deploy set it (in-process recreate: PreDeploy runs twice? env set on n.Cfg.Env which persists in-process!) — wait! In-process recreate: PreDeploy is called a second time on the same node instance. `ensureNumSlotsEnv` checks `n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] != ""` — after the first deploy set it, the second PreDeploy will skip recomputing! So if the user adds a link to a higher slot via `clab links apply` triggering recreate, CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS would NOT be updated because it's already set from the first deploy in the same process!

Wait — is PreDeploy called twice in one process for recreate? deployNode is called per node in deployApplyNodes during apply. If the node was deployed in the same process before apply... In `clab links apply`, the process is fresh (new CLI invocation) — c.Nodes freshly loaded, PreDeploy called once. But: are there in-process flows that deploy the same node twice? The apply flow: park → delete → deploy. Only one deploy per process. Fresh `clab deploy` — one per process. Hmm, but what about `clab deploy` then programmatic apply? CLI is one command per process. Actually, is there any path where both initial deploy and apply run in one process? No, they're separate commands.

But wait, actually — is the env-preservation check even the PR's change? `ensureNumSlotsEnv` and its early-return existed at base. Let me check base iol.go for ensureNumSlotsEnv.

Reasoning
Base matches head except the loop source and the boot_config guard. Good.

Now let's look at the `boot_config.txt` guard more carefully for a regression:

Old: PreDeploy always truncated `boot_config.txt`.
New: only create if missing.

When is the difference material? Consider `clab deploy --reconfigure`? In reconfigure, the lab dir is wiped, so file missing → created. Consider `clab redeploy`? Let me check what redeploy does — probably destroy+deploy, keeping lab dir. If the user runs `clab redeploy` (or apply-recreate) WITHOUT PostDeploy (skip-post-deploy), boot_config.txt retains the previous generated config — new behavior intends that (preserves config). 

But here's a scenario where the new guard could matter negatively: `GenBootConfig` writes `boot_config.txt` in PostDeploy. If the topology's `startup-config` is removed/changed between deploys, GenBootConfig overwrites → fine.

The real reason for the change (guessing): in the recreate flow, PreDeploy truncating boot_config.txt could wipe the IOL's saved startup config? Wait — where does IOL store its startup config? `wr` (write memory) writes to NVRAM (the nvram file, bind-mounted). boot_config.txt mounted as /iol/config.txt is the initial config loaded at boot. GenBootConfig overwrites it in PostDeploy anyway. So truncation at PreDeploy had no lasting effect in the normal flow — except between PreDeploy and PostDeploy the file was empty while the container may already boot? No, container starts after PreDeploy+Deploy; PostDeploy follows quickly but the VM may boot in between and read /iol/config.txt — if PreDeploy truncated it, IOL boots with EMPTY config! That's a real race: on redeploy of an existing IOL node, PreDeploy truncated boot_config.txt, then container starts; IOL reads empty config.txt → boots unconfigured (mgmt IP not set → mgmt broken, firstBoot logic says !firstBoot → UpdateMgmtIntf...). So the fix avoids booting with an empty config during recreate. Makes sense; the change is a genuine improvement. Good.

Now — potential issue with the boot_config guard: The bind mount comment. Fine.

(a) `EndpointsWithParked` — when parkingNode != nil, it returns a new slice each call. In `GenInterfaceConfig`, fine.

But the interfaces are still parked in the parking netns, and container creation... wait, does `clab deploy` on a stopped lab *recreate* containers? No — deploy detects existing containers and skips/starts them? Actually `clab deploy` on an existing stopped lab: nodes are "recreated"? Containerlab's deploy typically removes and recreates? No — deploy creates missing containers; if the container exists (stopped), deploy... hmm. Let me check: in deploy flow, does it destroy existing containers first? Historically `clab deploy` on an existing lab would fail or reuse? Modern clab deploy recreates nodes only if config changed? There's `--reconfigure` flag to force recreate. Without it, deploy skips existing nodes (logs "node already exists"?). Actually I believe modern containerlab `deploy` redeploys all nodes by default now? Hmm. In any case, the apply flow handles recreate with park; the deploy flow with existing containers — need to check whether the parking netns is considered. There's `RestoreEndpoints` called from where? node.Start (DefaultNode.Start) — after StartContainer. So if deploy just starts the stopped container (not recreate), then Start → RestoreEndpoints → interfaces moved back. But CLAB_INTFS: for a *started* (not recreated) container, env not re-set. Fine.

But wait — critical: if `clab deploy` recreates the container for a node whose interfaces are parked (e.g., after stop, user runs `clab deploy` which... does it park first?). Reasoning
OK. There's a whole "lab runtime" concept. This PR's changed code paths are narrow; the deep parking infrastructure is pre-existing.

Now, let me focus on validating the changed code more carefully for actual defects:

#### `EndpointsWithParked` — potential double-count after failed restore followed by redeploy in the same process

Scenario: `clab links apply` → park OK → container delete OK → deploy → StartContainer OK → restoreRecreatedNodes FAILS (e.g., Activate error). Now the apply returns error; some endpoints remain parked (parkingNode != nil), some endpoints were restored and are in d.Endpoints (AdoptEndpoint? no — moveEndpoint to dst adopts into dst.Endpoints). In RestoreTo, endpoints moved back are adopted into d via moveEndpoint. So after failed restore: d.Endpoints = restored subset; parkingNode = remaining. EndpointsWithParked = full set. Correct.

Now user re-runs `clab links apply` in the same... no, new process. Fresh process: parking netns exists, d.Endpoints from topology (full set). The plan sees node not recreated (running) → discoverLiveApplyEndpoints for recreated? Not in recreatedNodeSet in the new process... The parking netns is discovered via applyParkingLinkNode only for startNodeSet. This is pre-existing apply logic, not this PR.

#### Does `iol.Deploy` inherit DefaultNode.Deploy? iol doesn't override Deploy. Confirmed (no Deploy in iol.go).

A lone `///` between a tab and the note would end the tab group? Let me compare with base file to see what changed. The diff context shows:

The bare `///` presumably closes the tab-set wrapper (like `/// tab-container`? or just a blank separator that mkdocs uses to end the tabs group). #### What about `bridge`/`ovs-bridge`/`host` endpoints in EndpointsWithParked?

`d.Endpoints` includes ALL endpoints of the node, including `host`, `mgmt-net`, `bridge` endpoint types. Previously CLAB_INTFS = len(d.GetEndpoints()) included those too (pre-existing behavior — actually endpoints include every link endpoint). Hmm wait: CLAB_INTFS is meant to be the number of data interfaces added to the netns. Endpoints of type host (host interface, no veth created in the netns? Actually host endpoints bind an existing host interface INTO the container netns, so it does appear). Whatever — pre-existing semantics; the PR keeps the same set plus parked ones. Fine.

BUT — here's a subtle one: parked endpoints may include *runtime-discovered* endpoints created in the parking netns (`NewRuntimeEndpoint` in DiscoverOwnedInterfacesFor / captureCandidates). Those are extra interfaces discovered in the netns (e.g., interfaces created by the node itself at runtime, like IOL's... no). If the node had runtime-created interfaces (e.g., docker exec creating veths), parking captures them (with ownership altname check). Those count toward CLAB_INTFS — which is arguably correct since they'll be restored.

Hmm — but wait, in the recreate flow, captureCandidates only captures interfaces present in the container netns that are tracked or have the ownership altname. After parking, EndpointsWithParked = parked endpoints. Before this PR, CLAB_INTFS = len(d.Endpoints) which after parking was 0 (or fewer)! So on recreate, vrnetlab nodes got CLAB_INTFS=0 → VM started immediately with missing interfaces. The PR fixes that. Good.

#### Now — the `iol` NETMAP: the iouyap.ini maps `eth_dev = ethN` for each endpoint. Parked interfaces: when IOL container starts, the parked interfaces are NOT in its netns (they're in the parking netns). iouyap will try to open `ethN` device which doesn't exist → iouyap may fail to start or crash?! Hmm — but this is the whole point of the design: CLAB_INTFS tells vrnetlab to wait for all interfaces before starting the VM/iouyap. Restore happens shortly after container start. If iouyap starts before restore, it may fail. But wait — with CLAB_INTFS correct, vrnetlab waits for all interfaces to appear before launching the VM; once restore completes (interfaces moved into container netns), vrnetlab proceeds. The iouyap process inside the container presumably starts when the VM starts. There could be a race but the mechanism is designed around it (vrnetlab waits). The restore happens right after StartContainer returns... actually restoreRecreatedNodes runs after deployApplyNodes (all nodes deployed), and container start is async-ish? StartContainer waits for running state. So restore happens shortly after. vrnetlab's wait loop then sees all interfaces. Plausible design. Not something I can fully verify from source; it's the intended behavior of the PR.

Wait, actually, I realize I should double check: does iouyap get launched by IOL's entrypoint before or after CLAB_INTFS gate? Out of scope.

51: func (g *GenericLinkNode) AdoptEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
52: 	if e == nil {
53: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q cannot adopt a nil endpoint", g.shortname)
54: 	}
55: 
56: 	owner := e.GetNode()
57: 	if owner == nil {
58: 		return fmt.Errorf(
59: 			"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q without an owner",

62: 		)
63: 	}
64:

66: 		return fmt.Errorf(
67: 			"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q owned by %q",
68: 			g.shortname,
69: 			e.GetIfaceName(),

76: 			return nil

79: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q already tracks interface %q", g.shortname, e.GetIfaceName())
80: 		}
81: 	}
82: 
83: 	g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints, e)
84: 	return nil
85: }
86: 
87: func (g *GenericLinkNode) ReleaseEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
88: 	for i, ep := range g.endpoints {
89: 		if ep != e {
90: 			continue
91: 		}
92: 
93: 		g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints[:i], g.endpoints[i+1:]...)
94: 		return nil
95: 	}
96:

98: }

102: }

109: 	// The GenericLinkNode is the basis for Mgmt-Bridge and Host fake node.
110: 	// Both of these do generally exist. Hence the Deployed state in generally returned
111: 	return clabnodesstate.Deployed
112: }
113: 
114: func (g *GenericLinkNode) Delete(ctx context.Context) error {
115: 	for _, e := range g.endpoints {
116: 		var err error
117: 		if l := e.GetLink(); l != nil {
118: 			err = l.Remove(ctx)

122: 		if err != nil {
123: 			return err
124: 		}
125: 	}
